FILE: README.md
============================================================
# Dispatch: Autonomous Personal Content Engine

<p align="center">
  <img src="branding/dist/dispatch-lockup-horizontal-white.svg#gh-dark-mode-only" alt="Dispatch Logo" width="380" />
  <img src="branding/dist/dispatch-lockup-horizontal-black.svg#gh-light-mode-only" alt="Dispatch Logo" width="380" />
</p>

> **"You create the raw signal. Dispatch carries it the rest of the way."**

Dispatch is an autonomous, local-first content pipeline engineered to turn raw, multi-hour natural work and speaking sessions recorded on an Android phone into polished, high-retention vertical short-form videos (YouTube Shorts, Instagram Reels, LinkedIn, X).

Inspired by the "lead-miner" philosophy, Dispatch requires **zero manual intervention** once configured. There are no manual USB cable transfers, no timeline non-linear editing (NLE) drag-and-drops, and no multi-step render exports. 

---

## 1. System Philosophy: The 5-Step Human Loop

The system is built so the user only touches 5 high-value, tactile actions:
1. **Open Dispatch**: Open the app on your phone (POCO C65 or browser).
2. **Record**: Tap record when starting work or a speaking session.
3. **Stop**: Tap stop when you finish your session.
4. **Review Clips**: Open the Dispatch local dashboard on your PC at `http://localhost:8000`.
5. **Approve / Reject**: Tap **Approve** (or **Reject**). 
   - If set to **Auto-Publish**, the clip is automatically scheduled and posted publicly.
   - If set to **Private/Draft**, Dispatch uploads the clip as a draft or private post so you have total peace of mind and can inspect it on-platform before making it public.

**Everything between Stop and Review happens 100% autonomously in the background.**

---

## 2. Core Architecture: YouTube as Cloud Inbox

```text
📱 Phone (You)
   └── Record video → Stop → Auto-uploads to YouTube as Private
       (Tagged with durable dispatch_id in description/tags)
          │
          ▼
☁️ YouTube (Cloud Inbox)
   └── Stores raw video & generates automatic speech captions
          │
          ▼
💻 Laptop (Dispatch Catcher Daemon)
   └── Starts immediately on boot + polls every 10 min
       └── Discovers [DISPATCH] uploads matching dispatch_id
       └── Downloads the original video via yt-dlp
       └── Retrieves YouTube captions when available (local Whisper fallback)
       └── Sends the timestamped transcript to Gemini for clip selection
       └── FFmpeg cuts clips, formats 9:16 vertical video & burns ASS captions
          │
          ▼
🖥️ Review Dashboard (http://localhost:8000)
   └── Live clip preview → 1-click Approve → Dispatches across platforms
```

### Current architecture boundary
- The phone records and uploads directly to YouTube. It does not discover, pair with, or transfer video to the PC.
- The PC polls the authenticated YouTube account, downloads each Dispatch upload, processes it, and serves the review dashboard locally.
- If phone credentials are missing, the upload stays queued; there is no PC/LAN fallback.
- The phone uploads sessions to the owner's own channel as **unlisted**. NOTE (alpha policy): URL/manual ingestion accepts **public and unlisted** videos only; private videos are rejected by an explicit visibility gate before download (see `dispatch/youtube_inbox/catcher.py`). The owner's own poller flow is unaffected because phone sessions upload as unlisted.

---

## 3. Hardware & Operating Environment

- **Host Machine**: Windows 11 (AMD Ryzen / Intel CPU, 16GB+ RAM).
- **Compute Strategy**: 
  - Zero reliance on NVIDIA CUDA (optimized for AMD CPU multithreading via `faster-whisper` CPU-int8).
  - FFmpeg hardware acceleration where available, with fast CPU software fallback.
  - No heavyweight Docker/WSL2 containers to safeguard SSD space.
- **Mobile Hardware**: POCO C65 (Android 14+), recording 1080p @ 30 fps.
- **Budget & Cost**: 100% free local infrastructure; optional pennies spent strictly on Google Gemini Flash API for semantic highlight extraction.

---

## 4. Directory Structure

```
Dispatch/
├── README.md                     # Master project documentation
├── requirements.txt              # Standard Python dependencies
├── pyproject.toml                # Modern package configuration
├── dispatch/
│   ├── config.py                 # Central configurations & directory paths
│   ├── db.py                     # SQLite database schema, migrations & CRUD operations
│   ├── youtube_inbox/            # YouTube Cloud Inbox & Catcher
│   │   ├── catcher.py            # Catcher core: download -> captions -> clips
│   │   ├── vtt_parser.py         # WebVTT subtitle parser & word boundary estimator
│   │   ├── oauth.py              # YouTube Data API OAuth client
│   │   └── poller.py             # Background daemon polling for new uploads
│   ├── ingestion/                # File watcher & validator for incoming drops
│   ├── transcription/            # Speech extraction & Hinglish word alignment (faster-whisper)
│   ├── ai_clips/                 # Semantic highlight finder & preference learner (Gemini Flash)
│   ├── video_engine/             # FFmpeg 9:16 reframing, .ass subtitles & rendering
│   ├── web/                      # FastAPI review dashboard (PC & mobile)
│   │   ├── app.py
│   │   └── templates/
│   ├── publisher/                # Multi-platform publishing (YouTube, Instagram, LinkedIn, X)
│   ├── governor/                 # Hardware resource governor (CPU priority, battery, disk floor)
│   ├── orchestrator/             # Checkpointed pipeline runner & crash recovery
│   └── main.py                   # Master daemon entry point
├── android/                      # Native Android Studio Project (POCO C65)
│   ├── app/                      # CameraX recorder, Room DB, YouTubeDirectUploadWorker
│   ├── gradlew, gradlew.bat      # Self-contained Gradle wrapper scripts
│   └── build.gradle.kts
├── storage/
│   ├── incoming/                 # Raw video chunks
│   ├── youtube_inbox/            # Downloaded YouTube Inbox media
│   ├── processing/               # Active workspace during transcription/cutting
│   ├── clips/                    # Finished candidate clips ready for review
│   └── database/
│       └── dispatch.db           # SQLite state, checkpoints, & preference logs
└── tests/                        # Comprehensive unit, chaos, destructive & inbox tests (29 tests)
```

---

## 5. Quickstart & How to Run

### Step 1: Install Dependencies
```powershell
# Clone repo
git clone https://github.com/Resolviaai/Dispatch.git
cd Dispatch

# Install Python requirements
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

### Step 2: Configure Environment (.env)
Create a `.env` file in the project root:
```ini
GEMINI_API_KEY=your_gemini_api_key_here
DISPATCH_WEB_PORT=8000
DISPATCH_PUBLISH_MODE=private
DISPATCH_YOUTUBE_POLL_INTERVAL=600
```

### Step 3: Start Dispatch Daemon
```powershell
python -m dispatch.main
```
You will see:
```text
BOOTING DISPATCH: AUTONOMOUS PERSONAL CONTENT ENGINE
Database initialized
Autonomous YouTube Cloud Inbox poller active
Launching Web Dashboard on http://127.0.0.1:8000
```

### Step 4: Access Dashboard
Open **http://localhost:8000** in your browser.

---

## 6. Phone Recording Setup (POCO C65)

### Option A: Native Android App (Recommended)
1. Download the signed alpha APK from the public GitHub release: https://github.com/Resolviaai/Dispatch/releases/latest/download/Dispatch.apk (landing page: https://resolviaai.github.io/Dispatch/). The old dashboard route `/download/dispatch.apk` serves the same APK on your local network only and is not the public distribution path.
2. Install on your phone.
3. Features:
   - CameraX FHD 1080p video recording.
   - Pro Camera controls: Front/Back lens toggle, LED torch, AE/AF locking, tap-to-focus.
   - Background `RecordingForegroundService` keeps recording even when screen is locked.
   - `YouTubeDirectUploadWorker` automatically uploads recordings to YouTube as Private upon tapping Stop.
4. In **Settings**, enter the authorized account's YouTube OAuth refresh token, client ID, and client secret. The app stores them on the phone and uploads directly to YouTube; it does not pair with the PC.

The phone and PC operate independently after YouTube is configured. Review generated clips at `http://localhost:8000` on the PC.

---

## 7. Windows Silent Auto-Start on Boot

To run Dispatch silently in the background whenever Windows boots:
```cmd
scripts\install_windows_startup.bat
```
Registers Dispatch in Windows Task Scheduler to run hidden with below-normal CPU priority.
To uninstall at any time, run:
```cmd
scripts\uninstall_windows_startup.bat
```

---

## 8. Verification & Test Suite

Run the full automated test suite covering all 29 unit, integration, chaos, destructive, and YouTube inbox scenarios:

```powershell
# Run all 29 tests
python -m unittest discover tests

# Run YouTube Inbox tests
python -m unittest tests/test_youtube_inbox.py

# Run crash recovery & destructive tests
python -m unittest tests/test_destructive.py

# Run chaos engineering tests
python -m unittest tests/test_chaos.py
```
All 29 tests pass with 100% green exit code.

PHASE 2 — REPAIR TEXT ENTRY AND MOBILE KEYBOARD LAYOUT
The supplied screenshots show a text editing overlay that covers the video, a blue selection outline that becomes clipped, text extending outside the visible area, and a floating Done button that obstructs the preview.
2.1 Separate text entry from canvas manipulation
Implement a clear text-entry state.
Keep the text input separate from the actual video canvas.
When the user is editing text content, provide a dedicated input field with a clear confirmation action near the keyboard.
Keep live preview updates synchronized with the text being typed.
Do not place a large text-editing input box or floating Done button directly over the video.
Retain a separate application-level Done action only if it has a distinct exit or save purpose.
Avoid duplicated confirmation controls that perform indistinguishable actions.
Maintain the cursor position and text selection during typing, correction, and normal viewport resizing.
Do not steal focus, dismiss the keyboard, recreate the input, or reset the text when React state changes.
The text content editor and the direct canvas manipulation mode are distinct interactions. They must remain coordinated without sharing an incorrectly positioned input overlay.
2.2 Fix text clipping and wrapping
Fix the observed behavior where:
Enter caption text extends outside its visible container.
Entered text such as it's a new video is clipped.
The selected text bounding box extends beyond the preview.
The keyboard reduces the available preview area but the selection geometry does not adapt.
Text layout changes produce unexpected character spacing or incorrect selection handles.
Requirements:
Wrap text according to the actual content width, configured font, weight, size, and letter spacing.
Use the same font metrics and coordinate system when calculating the visible text and its selection bounds.
Keep selection handles attached to the rendered text.
Recalculate geometry after legitimate layout changes without resetting the user's chosen caption position.
Provide internal scrolling for longer input where necessary rather than clipping the entered value.
Handle empty strings, long words, spaces, newlines, and multiline text correctly.
Keep the active text visible and editable throughout keyboard interactions.
Do not silently reduce font size or change a user's style merely to force text into the viewport.
2.3 Handle Android WebView keyboard resizing correctly
Research reference: Android's [WebView window-insets documentation](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/layout/webapps/understand-window-insets).
Inspect the actual WebView and Android window configuration.

Recalculate the preview viewport and text selection geometry when the keyboard appears or disappears.

Make dismissal and confirmation return to a stable editor layout.
Modify Android window-inset configuration only when required by a verified issue. Do not introduce arbitrary delays or repeated keyboard focus calls as a workaround.

PHASE 3 — ENFORCE VALID TEXT-LAYER SELECTION
The user reported that Fonts and Styles appear active when no text layer is selected. Changing these controls then produces no visible change in the active editing view.

PHASE 5 — REBUILD PER-LINE BACKGROUNDS CORRECTLY
This is a critical rendering defect.
The supplied screenshot shows Per-Line selected, but the caption renders separate dark shapes and a blurred/glass-like appearance despite the user not enabling a blur option.
The public code contains a direct lead: the caption renderer and interactive canvas apply backdrop-filter: blur(8px) when background_enabled is true. The implementation must not use this implicit blur.
Research reference: [MDN — backdrop-filter](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/Properties/backdrop-filter).
5.1 Background modes must have distinct semantics
None
No text background.
Text, stroke, shadow, and explicitly enabled glow remain independent.
Per-Line
One continuous background rectangle for each actual rendered visual line.
The background must span the line's complete text content with consistent, configurable padding.
Do not put an independent rounded rectangle behind every word.
Do not generate one rounded box for every word or every active-word highlight.
Do not approximate this mode by placing a background around the entire multiline group.
Apply line spacing, padding, corner treatment, background color, and background opacity consistently.
The background must track the actual wrapped text lines rather than assuming every caption is a single line.
Active-word scaling and highlighting must not break the continuity of the background or create gaps between words.
When wrapping changes, update the line backgrounds without leaving stale shapes behind.
Full Box
Render one continuous background surrounding the complete caption text block.
The background should follow the entire multiline block rather than forming separate boxes behind each line or word.
Respect configured dimensions, padding, alignment, position, and corner radius.
5.2 Use real rendered line geometry
Inspect how text is currently laid out and wrapped in:
The direct-editing canvas.
Caption previews.
The playback caption renderer.
The text selection/bounding-box implementation.
The Python ASS exporter and final video-rendering path.
Implement a deterministic way of identifying the actual visual lines using the correct font resources, effective text style, available width, and layout configuration.
Use that line layout to position background shapes and to produce an equivalent result in video export.
Do not make the three modes differ only by changing a CSS class. They must produce visibly different and semantically correct output.
5.3 Remove implicit blur
Remove unconditional backdrop-filter: blur(8px) from caption backgrounds.
Do not activate blur merely because a background is enabled.
Do not confuse caption background rendering with the separate video Fit + Blur layout.
If an explicit background-blur control exists in the local workspace, render its setting only when that setting is enabled and persisted.
If no such control exists, do not invent one to justify the unwanted effect.
Keep background color and opacity independent of text-fill opacity.
Do not let changing background color or text opacity unintentionally alter shadow, glow, or the underlying video.
If the existing schema lacks a background mode, update the shared frontend and Python models and their serialization/deserialization paths. Use a backward-compatible migration/default that preserves existing saved projects as closely as possible.
Do not introduce a migration that silently disables existing backgrounds.
5.4 Preview and export must agree
Implement the same visible background semantics in:
The style preview.
The direct-editing canvas.
Playback.
Saved caption data.
Exported ASS and final video.
If native ASS styles cannot faithfully represent a required background mode, research and implement a supported rendering approach rather than leaving the UI setting cosmetic. Preserve word timing, active-word highlighting, and animation timing.

Confirm the backgrounds follow those lines.

PHASE 6 — REPAIR SUBTITLE/TEXT TIMELINE CARDS
The supplied videos show numerous compact subtitle/text cards across the timeline. The cards appear cramped, overlapped, inconsistent, and difficult to read or manipulate. The user also reports glitches and unreliable interactions.
Inspect CaptionTimeline.tsx and the actual local rendering of subtitle groups and text layers.
Current public-code issues to investigate
The public implementation calculates each block's visual width using a minimum width of 32 pixels and places both trim handles, an icon, a label, and selected-duration metadata inside that width. When actual caption durations are short, the contents do not fit.
The timeline's visual representation must be rebuilt around accurate media timing and usable touch interactions.
Required behavior
Accurate dimensions
Derive a card's horizontal position and visual width from its actual start time, end time, timeline zoom, and scroll offset.
Do not extend the visible duration just to fit its label.
Do not let a minimum-width styling rule create false temporal overlap between adjacent subtitles.
Preserve correct time mapping during zoom, scroll, trim, move, seek, and scrubbing.
Any enlarged touch target must not interfere with selecting adjacent caption cards.
Compact card design
Keep card height and corner radius consistent within each track.
Keep selected, active, hovered, and inactive states visually distinct.
Remove forced content that cannot fit inside narrow cards.
Show a title only when the available width supports it.
Truncate safely where needed.
Show duration metadata only when it fits.
Hide or collapse nonessential labels and icons on short blocks instead of clipping them.
Display trim handles only when practical, such as for a selected block or when sufficient width is available.
Keep touch targets usable on mobile even when the visible card is narrow.
Do not fill the timeline with excessive borders, labels, or decorative elements.
Preserve the existing track hierarchy and the intended visual relationship between Main, L1, L2, and other tracks.
Handle overlapping intervals correctly
Distinguish a true time overlap from false overlap caused by minimum-width styling.
Ensure adjacent short caption blocks remain individually selectable.
If actual intervals overlap, represent that situation deliberately rather than letting invisible hit areas select the wrong card.
Preserve intentional subtitle timing and text grouping.
Drag, trim, and selection correctness
Tapping a card selects the correct caption group.
Dragging moves only the selected group.
Start and end handles modify only their corresponding boundaries.
Pointer cancellation and touch interruption cannot leave a drag operation stuck.
The playhead remains aligned with the correct timestamp.
A timeline scroll must not accidentally seek the video repeatedly during automatic playhead-following.
Undo/redo and persistence must retain the correct caption timing.
Do not change caption segmentation or merge word groups merely to improve appearance. Preserve the actual transcript/timing model unless a demonstrated data defect requires a separate change.
Acceptance test with many short subtitle groups, adjacent groups, overlapping groups, long text labels, zoomed-out view, zoomed-in view, and selected/unselected cards.
PHASE 7 — INVESTIGATE AND FIX PLAYBACK STUTTER, LAG, AND PAUSES
Treat this as a real performance investigation. The reported behavior includes stuttering, lag, and occasions where playback appears to pause.
Do not assume a single cause. Reproduce and instrument the media stage before selecting the fix.
7.1 Establish the baseline
Using the same representative clip, record what the current build does.
Where the environment permits, measure:
Time from clip selection to visible poster/preview.
Time until the first decoded video frame can be displayed.
Unexpected pauses, buffering, and stalls.
Video current-time progression and caption synchronization.
Dropped-frame metrics where supported.
Timeline playhead movement during uninterrupted playback.
Excessive React rerenders and long main-thread tasks.
Network or media requests that compete with selected-video playback.
Memory and CPU pressure, particularly on the POCO C65.
Use media events such as playing, waiting, stalled, seeking, seeked, pause, ended, and error to distinguish intentional pauses from buffering or broken playback.
Do not infer a dropped frame from an arbitrary repeated screen-recording frame. Collect evidence from the real application.
7.2 Remove competing video work
Inspect FramedVideoStage and both foreground/background video elements.
Do not render or decode an unnecessary blurred-background video in layouts that do not use Fit + Blur.
Do not play a hidden background video when its layout mode is inactive.
Mount or activate the separate background video only when needed.
When Fit + Blur is active, keep the foreground and background layers synchronized during play, pause, seek, clip start, and clip end.
Do not repeatedly reset media time in response to small synchronization differences.
Investigate play() failures and buffering instead of silently swallowing every rejected promise.
Avoid unnecessary decoding and network work for unselected clips.
Do not preload every video.
Keep the selected video's loading path fast while retaining the intended appearance of every layout mode.
7.3 Fix the timeline animation loop
The public CaptionTimeline.tsx has a playhead-following requestAnimationFrame effect whose dependencies include currentTime. That is a confirmed candidate for repeated loop restart during playback.
Keep the animation loop stable while the media plays.
Use refs for frequently changing media time where appropriate.
Avoid restarting the loop on every time update.
Use requestVideoFrameCallback when supported and use a reliable animation-frame fallback otherwise.
Clean up scheduled callbacks on pause, unmount, clip change, and relevant interaction changes.
Prevent multiple overlapping loops.
Stop automatic follow behavior while the user is dragging or intentionally scrubbing.
Resume normal follow behavior after the interaction ends.
Reference: [MDN — HTMLVideoElement.requestVideoFrameCallback](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/HTMLVideoElement/requestVideoFrameCallback).
7.4 Reduce playback-driven React work
Inspect all state changes triggered by onTimeUpdate.
Do not force the entire editor and timeline to rerender on every video frame when only the playhead or spoken word state needs to change.
Use narrowly scoped updates for high-frequency media state.
Keep the active-word color/animation synchronized with actual playback timestamps.
Avoid expensive line layout, font measurement, filmstrip generation, and background calculations on every time update.
Cache or reuse deterministic layouts where safe; invalidate them when text, font, width, or style genuinely changes.
Keep performance optimizations subordinate to correctness.
7.5 Investigate the reported unexpected pauses
Trace the playback state machine and clip timebase mapping.
Identify all intentional pause() calls.
Verify clip-start and clip-end bounds.
Check source-time versus pre-rendered-clip time mapping.
Ensure a seek does not set the application state to an inconsistent time.
Verify the foreground and optional background videos do not trigger competing state changes.
Handle failed play() promises and media errors explicitly.
Ensure loading or waiting events are not treated as user-requested pauses.
Check that a previous clip's event handlers cannot update the state of a newly selected clip.
Do not remove legitimate end-of-clip behavior. Playback should stop at the intended end and should resume correctly when the user presses Play again.
7.6 Measure the result
Repeat the baseline test with the same media and comparable device conditions.
Target:
No unexplained pauses or repeated play/pause transitions.
No visible caption updates ahead of the corresponding video frame.
Stable playhead movement during uninterrupted playback.
No unnecessary background-video decoding outside Fit + Blur.
No excessive full-editor rerendering attributable to the timeline.
No regression in seeking, scrubbing, looping, or selected-caption styling.
Report numeric metrics where available. Do not promise a particular frame rate on an unsupported device/browser configuration; report the measured behavior and the exact environment.
PHASE 8 — PRESERVE AND VERIFY THE EXISTING CAPTION EDITOR REQUIREMENTS
These requirements remain part of this repair where the corresponding defects still exist locally.

PHASE 9 — SHARED MODELS, PERSISTENCE, AND VIDEO EXPORT

PHASE 11 — REGRESSION TESTS AND ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
Run existing tests and add focused regression coverage.
Text editor

Relevant frontend tests pass.

Physical-device verification is reported separately from build verification.
FINAL REPORT
When finished, provide:
Each confirmed issue and its root cause.
The implementation changes and affected files.
What was measured before and after, particularly playback stutter and timeline behavior.
Results of relevant automated tests and actual build commands.
Whether the bundled frontend assets and APK were rebuilt.

Remaining failures or anything that could not be verified.

FILE: scripts\auth_youtube.py
============================================================
"""Run the desktop YouTube OAuth flow and report safe status updates to Dispatch."""
import json
import os
import socket
import sys
import tempfile
import urllib.error
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, Optional

ROOT_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
CLIENT_SECRETS_FILE = ROOT_DIR / "client_secrets.json"
TOKEN_FILE = ROOT_DIR / "youtube_token.json"
STATE_PREFIX = "DISPATCH_OAUTH_STATE="

SCOPES = [
    "https://www.googleapis.com/auth/youtube",
    "https://www.googleapis.com/auth/youtube.upload",
    "https://www.googleapis.com/auth/youtube.readonly",
]


def emit_state(status: str, message: str, **details: Any) -> None:
    """Emit one machine-readable, non-secret state line for the parent process."""
    payload: Dict[str, Any] = {"status": status, "message": message}
    payload.update(details)
    print(STATE_PREFIX + json.dumps(payload, separators=(",", ":")), flush=True)


def _is_network_error(error: BaseException) -> bool:
    pending = [error]
    seen = set()
    while pending:
        current = pending.pop()
        if id(current) in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(id(current))
        if isinstance(current, (socket.gaierror, TimeoutError, ConnectionError, urllib.error.URLError)):
            return True
        reason = getattr(current, "reason", None)
        if isinstance(reason, BaseException):
            pending.append(reason)
        for nested in (current.__cause__, current.__context__):
            if isinstance(nested, BaseException):
                pending.append(nested)
    return False


def _save_token_atomically(token_json: str) -> None:
    """Write a completed token without replacing an existing token on partial failure."""
    fd, temp_name = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix="youtube_token_", suffix=".tmp", dir=ROOT_DIR)
    temp_path = Path(temp_name)
    try:
        with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as token_file:
            token_file.write(token_json)
            token_file.flush()
            os.fsync(token_file.fileno())
        os.replace(temp_path, TOKEN_FILE)
    finally:
        try:
            temp_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
        except OSError:
            pass


def run_authentication() -> Optional[Dict[str, str]]:
    """Complete OAuth, verify the resulting token against YouTube, then save it."""
    if not CLIENT_SECRETS_FILE.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError("client_secrets.json is missing from the Dispatch folder.")

    try:
        from google_auth_oauthlib.flow import InstalledAppFlow
        from googleapiclient.discovery import build
    except ImportError as error:
        raise RuntimeError(
            "The active Dispatch Python environment is missing Google OAuth packages. "
            "Run python -m pip install -r requirements.txt with the same Python executable."
        ) from error

    flow = InstalledAppFlow.from_client_secrets_file(str(CLIENT_SECRETS_FILE), scopes=SCOPES)
    emit_state("awaiting_authorization", "Complete Google sign-in in the browser opened by Dispatch.")
    credentials = flow.run_local_server(
        port=0,
        open_browser=True,
        access_type="offline",
        prompt="consent",
    )

    if not credentials.refresh_token:
        raise RuntimeError("Google did not return a refresh token. Revoke Dispatch access and try again.")

    service = build("youtube", "v3", credentials=credentials, cache_discovery=False)
    response = service.channels().list(part="snippet", mine=True, maxResults=1).execute()
    channels = response.get("items", [])
    if not channels:
        raise RuntimeError("Google authorization succeeded, but no YouTube channel was returned.")

    _save_token_atomically(credentials.to_json())
    channel = channels[0]
    return {
        "channel_title": str(channel.get("snippet", {}).get("title", "")),
        "channel_id": str(channel.get("id", "")),
    }


def main() -> int:
    emit_state("starting", "Starting Google authorization.")
    try:
        channel = run_authentication()
        emit_state("authenticated", "YouTube account connected and verified.", **(channel or {}))
        print("YouTube OAuth completed and verified.", flush=True)
        return 0
    except Exception as error:
        if _is_network_error(error):
            message = "Google could not be reached due to a DNS or network error. Check the PC connection and retry."
        elif isinstance(error, FileNotFoundError):
            message = str(error)
        elif isinstance(error, RuntimeError):
            message = str(error)
        else:
            message = "Google authorization failed. Check the OAuth client setup and retry."
        emit_state("failed", message)
        print("YouTube OAuth failed: " + message, file=sys.stderr, flush=True)
        return 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

FILE: scripts\get_code.py
============================================================
"""Fetch and display a fresh 5-minute Dispatch phone pairing code."""
import json
import urllib.request
import urllib.error
import sys

def main():
    url = "http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/integrations/youtube/pairing-code"
    data = json.dumps({"understands_http_risk": True}).encode("utf-8")
    req = urllib.request.Request(
        url,
        data=data,
        headers={"Host": "127.0.0.1:8000", "Content-Type": "application/json"}
    )
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(req) as resp:
            result = json.loads(resp.read().decode("utf-8"))
            code = result.get("code")
            print("=" * 45)
            print(f"  DISPATCH PAIRING CODE:  {code}")
            print(f"  Valid for: 5 minutes")
            print("=" * 45)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
        print(f"Error ({e.code}): {e.read().decode('utf-8')}", file=sys.stderr)
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Failed to connect to Dispatch server: {e}", file=sys.stderr)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

FILE: scripts\install_windows_startup.bat
============================================================
@echo off
REM ============================================================================
REM Dispatch: Windows Automatic Background Startup Installer
REM Registers Dispatch in Windows Task Scheduler to start silently on boot/logon.
REM Runs with low priority so it never lags games, browser, or foreground apps.
REM ============================================================================

set SCRIPT_DIR=%~dp0
set VBS_PATH=%SCRIPT_DIR%run_background.vbs

echo [*] Registering Dispatch Background Service with Windows Task Scheduler...
schtasks /create /tn "DispatchAutonomousEngine" /tr "wscript.exe \"%VBS_PATH%\"" /sc onlogon /rl limited /f

if %ERRORLEVEL% equ 0 (
    echo [SUCCESS] Dispatch will now automatically start silently whenever you log in!
    echo [INFO] Resource governor enforces below-normal CPU priority and battery preservation.
) else (
    echo [ERROR] Failed to register task with error code %ERRORLEVEL%.
)
pause

============================================================
FILE: scripts\run_background.vbs
============================================================
' Dispatch Background Silent Launcher (Zero Terminal Popups)
Set WshShell = CreateObject("WScript.Shell")
Set FSO = CreateObject("Scripting.FileSystemObject")

' Current directory resolution
strScriptPath = FSO.GetParentFolderName(WScript.ScriptFullName)
strProjectRoot = FSO.GetParentFolderName(strScriptPath)

' Check for virtual environment python
strVenvPython = strProjectRoot & "\.venv\Scripts\python.exe"
If FSO.FileExists(strVenvPython) Then
    strPyExe = """" & strVenvPython & """"
Else
    strPyExe = "python"
End If

' Ensure storage directory exists for logs
strStorageDir = strProjectRoot & "\storage"
If Not FSO.FolderExists(strStorageDir) Then
    FSO.CreateFolder(strStorageDir)
End If

strLogFile = strStorageDir & "\dispatch_daemon.log"
WshShell.CurrentDirectory = strProjectRoot

' Launch Python dispatch master daemon silently with stderr/stdout redirection
strCommand = "cmd.exe /c " & strPyExe & " -m dispatch.main >> """ & strLogFile & """ 2>&1"
WshShell.Run strCommand, 0, False

netsh interface ipv4 set address name="Wi-Fi" static 192.168.0.200 255.255.255.0 192.168.0.1

netsh interface ipv4 show addresses "Wi-Fi"
echo.
echo Your permanent Dispatch URL is now:
echo http://192.168.0.200:8000/download/dispatch.apk
echo ========================================================
pause

FILE: scripts\test_clip_preview.py
============================================================
"""Interactive test script to preview clip quality and active-word subtitle rendering.
Takes an existing video (or default speech sample), transcribes with Whisper,
detects highlights, and renders a vertical 9:16 video with karaoke subtitles.
"""
import sys
import os
from pathlib import Path

# Add project root to path
ROOT_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT_DIR))

from dispatch.transcription.transcriber import transcribe_video
from dispatch.ai_clips.highlight_finder import extract_clips_gemini, extract_clips_local_heuristic
from dispatch.video_engine.renderer import render_clip
from dispatch.config import CLIPS_DIR, PROCESSING_DIR, GEMINI_API_KEY


def run_preview(video_path: Path):
    print("=" * 65)
    print("DISPATCH CLIP & SUBTITLE QUALITY PREVIEW")
    print("=" * 65)
    print(f"Input Video : {video_path}")
    print(f"Video Size  : {video_path.stat().st_size / (1024*1024):.2f} MB")
    
    # 1. Transcription
    print("\n[Step 1/3] Running Whisper Speech-to-Text (local int8 CPU multithreaded)...")
    trans_res = transcribe_video(video_path)
    segments = trans_res.get("segments", [])
    full_text = trans_res.get("full_text", "")
    print(f"   Detected Language : {trans_res.get('language')}")
    print(f"   Total Segments    : {len(segments)}")
    print(f"   Transcript Preview: \"{full_text[:120]}...\"")

    if not segments:
        print("[ERROR] No speech segments detected.")
        return

    # Total duration from last segment
    total_duration = segments[-1]["end"]

    # 2. Highlight Detection
    print("\n[Step 2/3] Evaluating Quality & Highlight Detection...")
    gemini_clips = None
    if GEMINI_API_KEY:
        print("   Attempting Gemini Cloud Analysis...")
        gemini_clips = extract_clips_gemini(full_text, segments)

    if gemini_clips:
        print("   [INFO] Highlight selected by Gemini Cloud AI")
        clips = gemini_clips
    else:
        print("   [INFO] Gemini API Key not active/set. Using Local Heuristic Engine...")
        clips = extract_clips_local_heuristic(segments, total_duration)

    if not clips:
        # Fallback to entire clip
        clips = [{
            "start_time": 0.0,
            "end_time": total_duration,
            "title": "Dispatch Preview Highlight",
            "virality_score": 85,
            "layout_recommendation": "fit_blur"
        }]

    clip = clips[0]
    start_t = clip["start_time"]
    end_t = clip["end_time"]
    title = clip.get("title", "Dispatch Preview Clip")
    virality = clip.get("virality_score", 80)
    layout = clip.get("layout_recommendation", "fit_blur")

    print(f"   Selected Clip : \"{title}\"")
    print(f"   Time Window   : {start_t:.1f}s -> {end_t:.1f}s (Duration: {end_t - start_t:.1f}s)")
    print(f"   Virality Score: {virality}/100")
    print(f"   Framing Mode  : {layout}")

    # 3. Video Rendering with Subtitles
    print("\n[Step 3/3] Rendering 9:16 Vertical Video with Karaoke Subtitles via FFmpeg...")
    clip_id = "test_render_preview"
    output = render_clip(
        source_video=video_path,
        clip_id=clip_id,
        start_time=start_t,
        end_time=end_t,
        aspect_ratio="16:9",
        layout_mode=layout,
        segments=segments
    )

    rendered_path = output["video_path"]
    thumb_path = output["thumbnail_path"]

    print("\n" + "=" * 65)
    print("PREVIEW RENDER COMPLETE!")
    print("=" * 65)
    print(f"Rendered Video : {rendered_path.resolve()}")
    if thumb_path:
        print(f"Thumbnail      : {thumb_path.resolve()}")
    print("\nTo view it immediately, open PowerShell and run:")
    print(f"   start \"{rendered_path.resolve()}\"")
    print("=" * 65)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    target = None
    if len(sys.argv) > 1:
        target = Path(sys.argv[1])
    else:
        default_video = ROOT_DIR / "storage" / "processing" / "phone_sync_chunk_speech.mp4"
        if default_video.exists():
            target = default_video

    if not target or not target.exists():
        print("Please provide a video file path: python scripts/test_clip_preview.py <path_to_video.mp4>")
        sys.exit(1)

    run_preview(target)

  <section id="install">
    <h2>Installing the app</h2>
    <div class="faq">
      <p class="q">Android blocked the install with "unknown sources"</p>
      <p class="a">That is expected for any app outside the Play Store. Tap <strong>Settings → Allow from this source</strong> in the dialog (it only trusts this one download), then tap Install again. If it says <em>"App not installed"</em> and you previously installed an early test build of Dispatch, first uninstall the old app (long-press its icon → Uninstall) and install this download again — the first public build uses a new secure signature, and Android never updates across different signatures. Uninstalling clears the old app’s local data, so re-pair it with your PC afterwards.</p>
      <p class="q">Where is the Windows companion?</p>
      <p class="a">Clone or download <a class="link" href="https://github.com/Resolviaai/Dispatch" target="_blank" rel="noopener">the Dispatch repository</a> on your PC and follow the README setup steps. The phone app needs the PC companion running on your Wi-Fi to process sessions.</p>
      <p class="q">Something broke — who do I ask?</p>
      <p class="a">Open an issue on <a class="link" href="https://github.com/Resolviaai/Dispatch/issues" target="_blank" rel="noopener">the GitHub issue tracker</a> and include what you were doing plus any error text.</p>
    </div>
  </section>

import { CaptionTrack, CaptionGroup } from './frontend/src/captions/types';
import { extractSectionProperties, applySectionStylesToTrack } from './frontend/src/captions/applyToAll';

console.log('🧪 Starting Dispatch "Apply to All Text Styling" Test Suite...\n');

// Mock Caption Track
const initialTrack: CaptionTrack = {
  version: 2,

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// TEST 1: Property Isolation — Font Section
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
console.log('Test 1: Font section isolation...');
{
  const result = applySectionStylesToTrack(initialTrack, 'cap_b', 'font');
  assert.strictEqual(result.affectedCount, 3, 'Should affect exactly 3 text/caption elements');

  const capA = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_a')!;
  const capB = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_b')!;
  const capC = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_c')!;

  // Font properties must match cap_b
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.font_family, 'Montserrat');
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.font_size, 48);
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.font_weight, 'bold');
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.letter_spacing, 1.5);
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.line_height, 1.3);
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.text_transform, 'uppercase');

  assert.strictEqual(capC.style?.font_family, 'Montserrat');
  assert.strictEqual(capC.style?.font_size, 48);

  // UNRELATED properties must NOT be changed!
  // cap_a had primary_color #FFFFFF and outline_color #111111
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.primary_color, '#FFFFFF', 'Cap A color must remain intact');
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.outline_enabled, true, 'Cap A outline must remain intact');
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.outline_color, '#111111', 'Cap A outline color must remain intact');
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.shadow_enabled, false, 'Cap A shadow must remain false');

  // cap_c had primary_color #00FFFF, glow_enabled true, background_enabled true
  assert.strictEqual(capC.style?.primary_color, '#00FFFF', 'Cap C color must remain intact');
  assert.strictEqual(capC.style?.glow_enabled, true, 'Cap C glow must remain intact');
  assert.strictEqual(capC.style?.background_enabled, true, 'Cap C background must remain intact');
  assert.strictEqual(capC.style?.outline_color, '#FF0000', 'Cap C outline color must remain intact');

  console.log('  ✓ Font properties propagated without touching colors, strokes, glows, or shadows');
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// TEST 2: Property Isolation — Color Section
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
console.log('Test 2: Color section isolation...');
{
  const result = applySectionStylesToTrack(initialTrack, 'cap_c', 'color');
  assert.strictEqual(result.affectedCount, 3);

  const capA = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_a')!;
  const capB = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_b')!;
  const capC = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_c')!;

  // Primary color must now match cap_c
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.primary_color, '#00FFFF');
  assert.strictEqual(capB.style?.primary_color, '#00FFFF');
  assert.strictEqual(capC.style?.primary_color, '#00FFFF');

  // But font properties on cap_b must remain Montserrat Bold 48!
  assert.strictEqual(capB.style?.font_family, 'Montserrat', 'Cap B font must remain untouched');
  assert.strictEqual(capB.style?.font_size, 48, 'Cap B font size must remain untouched');
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.font_family, 'Inter', 'Cap A font must remain untouched');

  console.log('  ✓ Color applied to all without touching font families or sizes');
}

  const audio = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'audio_layer')!;

}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// TEST 5: One-Time Action & Future Edits Independence
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
console.log('Test 5: One-time action verification (future edits remain local)...');
{
  // Step A: Apply font to all
  const afterApply = applySectionStylesToTrack(initialTrack, 'cap_b', 'font').nextTrack;

  // Step B: Later routine edit on Caption C ONLY (simulating CaptionStudio patch())
  const targetGroup = afterApply.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_c')!;
  const localEditedTrack: CaptionTrack = {
    ...afterApply,
    groups: afterApply.groups.map((grp) => {
      if (grp.id !== targetGroup.id) return grp;
      return {
        ...grp,
        style: { ...(grp.style || afterApply.default_style), primary_color: '#990099' },
      };
    }),
  };

  const capA = localEditedTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_a')!;
  const capB = localEditedTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_b')!;
  const capC = localEditedTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_c')!;

  assert.strictEqual(capC.style?.primary_color, '#990099', 'Cap C changed color');
  assert.strictEqual(capA.style?.primary_color, '#FFFFFF', 'Cap A color was NOT changed by future edit');
  assert.strictEqual(capB.style?.primary_color, '#FF0055', 'Cap B color was NOT changed by future edit');

  console.log('  ✓ Routine edits remain strictly local; Apply to All is not a persistent mode');
}

console.log('Test 6: Stroke, Glow, Background, Shadow, Alignment, and Animation isolation...');
{
  // Stroke test
  const strokeResult = applySectionStylesToTrack(initialTrack, 'cap_a', 'stroke').nextTrack;
  const strokeCapB = strokeResult.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_b')!;
  assert.strictEqual(strokeCapB.style?.outline_enabled, true);
  assert.strictEqual(strokeCapB.style?.outline_color, '#111111');
  assert.strictEqual(strokeCapB.style?.outline_width, 2);
  assert.strictEqual(strokeCapB.style?.font_family, 'Montserrat', 'Font untouched during stroke apply');

  // Alignment test
  const alignTrack: CaptionTrack = {
    ...initialTrack,
    groups: initialTrack.groups.map((g) =>
      g.id === 'cap_c' ? { ...g, layout: { ...initialTrack.default_layout, alignment: 'right' } } : g
    ),
  };
  const alignResult = applySectionStylesToTrack(alignTrack, 'cap_c', 'alignment').nextTrack;
  const alignCapA = alignResult.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_a')!;
  assert.strictEqual(alignCapA.layout?.alignment, 'right');
  assert.strictEqual(alignCapA.style?.font_family, 'Inter', 'Style untouched during alignment apply');

  console.log('  ✓ Stroke, alignment, and other individual sections propagate strictly isolated properties');
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// TEST 7: Timeline-Aware Visibility Invariant
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
console.log('Test 7: Timeline-aware visibility invariant (selection does NOT force visibility outside duration)...');
{
  const groups: CaptionGroup[] = [
    { id: 'c1', start: 0, end: 2.0, words: [{ id: 'w1', text: 'One', start: 0, end: 2.0 }] },
    { id: 'c2', start: 2.5, end: 4.5, words: [{ id: 'w2', text: 'Two', start: 2.5, end: 4.5 }] },
  ];

  const isVisible = (grp: CaptionGroup, currentTime: number) => {
    return currentTime >= grp.start && currentTime <= grp.end;
  };

  // Playhead at 1.0 (inside c1, outside c2)
  assert.strictEqual(isVisible(groups[0], 1.0), true, 'c1 must be visible at t=1.0');
  assert.strictEqual(isVisible(groups[1], 1.0), false, 'c2 must be invisible at t=1.0');

  // Even if c2 is selected, its timeline-aware visibility remains false!
  const selectedGroupId = 'c2';
  const c2IsSelected = selectedGroupId === groups[1].id;
  assert.strictEqual(c2IsSelected, true, 'c2 is selected');
  assert.strictEqual(isVisible(groups[1], 1.0), false, 'c2 selection must NOT force it visible at t=1.0');

  // Playhead at 3.0 (outside c1, inside c2)
  assert.strictEqual(isVisible(groups[0], 3.0), false, 'c1 must be invisible at t=3.0');
  assert.strictEqual(isVisible(groups[1], 3.0), true, 'c2 must be visible at t=3.0');
  console.log('  ✓ Layer visibility is strictly timeline-aware; selection never forces visibility outside duration');
}

console.log('Test 8: Categorized animation model and loop propagation on Apply to All...');
{
  const animTrack: CaptionTrack = {
    ...initialTrack,
    groups: initialTrack.groups.map((g) =>
      g.id === 'cap_a'
        ? {
            ...g,
            animation: {
              type: 'pop',
              duration_ms: 100,
              active_scale: 1.2,
              easing: 'bounce',
              enter: { type: 'slide_up', duration_ms: 220 },
              exit: { type: 'slide_down', duration_ms: 160 },
              loop: { type: 'pulse', speed_ms: 850 },
              active_word: { type: 'pop', active_scale: 1.25, duration_ms: 110 },
            },
          }
        : g
    ),
  };

  const animResult = applySectionStylesToTrack(animTrack, 'cap_a', 'animation').nextTrack;
  const capBAnim = animResult.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_b')?.animation;
  assert.strictEqual(capBAnim?.enter?.type, 'slide_up');
  assert.strictEqual(capBAnim?.exit?.type, 'slide_down');
  assert.strictEqual(capBAnim?.loop?.type, 'pulse');
  assert.strictEqual(capBAnim?.loop?.speed_ms, 850);
  assert.strictEqual(capBAnim?.active_word?.active_scale, 1.25);

  // Check that cap_b font and colors were NOT affected
  const capBStyle = animResult.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_b')?.style;
  assert.strictEqual(capBStyle?.font_family, 'Montserrat', 'Font preserved on animation apply');
  assert.strictEqual(capBStyle?.primary_color, '#FF0055', 'Color preserved on animation apply');

  console.log('  ✓ Animation enter, exit, loop, and active_word propagate accurately with complete style isolation');
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// TEST 9: Apply Position & Size to All Captions (Layout Section Isolation)
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
console.log('Test 9: Apply Position & Size to All Captions (layout section isolation & layer restriction)...');
{
  const layoutTrack: CaptionTrack = {
    ...initialTrack,
    groups: [
      ...initialTrack.groups,
      {
        id: 'manual_title',
        type: 'text',
        start: 0,
        end: 5.0,
        layout: {
          position_x: 0.1,
          position_y: 0.15,
          scale: 0.8,
          alignment: 'left',
          max_width: 0.6,
          max_lines: 1,
        },
        style: {
          font_family: 'Arial Black',
          font_size: 20,
          font_weight: 'bold',
          primary_color: '#FF0000',
        },
        words: [{ id: 'mt1', text: 'Top Title', start: 0, end: 5.0 }],
      },
    ],
  };

  // Modify layout of cap_b locally (drag / resize on preview)
  const modifiedTrack: CaptionTrack = {
    ...layoutTrack,
    groups: layoutTrack.groups.map((g) =>
      g.id === 'cap_b'
        ? {
            ...g,
            layout: {
              position_x: 0.42,
              position_y: 0.82,
              scale: 1.35,
              alignment: 'center',
              max_width: 0.9,
              max_lines: 2,
            },
          }
        : g
    ),
  };

  // Verify that prior to clicking "Apply to All", other captions remain UNCHANGED
  const preCapA = modifiedTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_a')!;
  assert.strictEqual(preCapA.layout?.position_y, undefined, 'Local change must not leak before Apply to All');

  // Trigger Apply to All Captions with section: 'layout'
  const result = applySectionStylesToTrack(modifiedTrack, 'cap_b', 'layout');

  // Must affect ONLY the 3 spoken captions (cap_a, cap_b, cap_c)
  assert.strictEqual(result.affectedCount, 3, 'Must affect strictly spoken captions');

  const postCapA = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_a')!;
  const postCapB = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_b')!;
  const postCapC = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_c')!;
  const postManualText = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'manual_title')!;
  const postOverlay = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'overlay_img')!;
  const postAudio = result.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'audio_layer')!;

  // 1. Spoken captions received the new position & scale
  assert.strictEqual(postCapA.layout?.position_x, 0.42, 'cap_a received position_x');
  assert.strictEqual(postCapA.layout?.position_y, 0.82, 'cap_a received position_y');
  assert.strictEqual(postCapA.layout?.scale, 1.35, 'cap_a received scale');

  assert.strictEqual(postCapC.layout?.position_x, 0.42, 'cap_c received position_x');
  assert.strictEqual(postCapC.layout?.position_y, 0.82, 'cap_c received position_y');
  assert.strictEqual(postCapC.layout?.scale, 1.35, 'cap_c received scale');

  // 2. Unrelated properties PRESERVED (styles, animations, words, timestamps)
  assert.strictEqual(postCapA.style?.font_family, 'Inter', 'cap_a font preserved');
  assert.strictEqual(postCapA.style?.primary_color, '#FFFFFF', 'cap_a color preserved');
  assert.strictEqual(postCapA.words[0].style_override?.color, '#00FF00', 'cap_a word override preserved');
  assert.strictEqual(postCapC.style?.glow_enabled, true, 'cap_c glow preserved');

  // 3. Manual text (type === 'text') is STRICTLY IMMUNE to caption layout changes!
  assert.strictEqual(postManualText.layout?.position_y, 0.15, 'Manual text layout must NOT be modified');
  assert.strictEqual(postManualText.layout?.position_x, 0.1, 'Manual text layout must NOT be modified');
  assert.strictEqual(postManualText.layout?.scale, 0.8, 'Manual text scale must NOT be modified');

  // 4. Overlays and audio are strictly immune
  assert.strictEqual(postOverlay.layout?.position_x, undefined, 'Overlay layout not modified');
  assert.strictEqual(postAudio.type, 'audio', 'Audio layer not modified');

  // 5. Future local edits remain independent (one-time action, not persistent mode)
  const futureEditTrack: CaptionTrack = {
    ...result.nextTrack,
    groups: result.nextTrack.groups.map((g) =>
      g.id === 'cap_a'
        ? { ...g, layout: { ...g.layout!, position_y: 0.5 } }
        : g
    ),
  };
  const capAFuture = futureEditTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_a')!;
  const capBFuture = futureEditTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_b')!;
  assert.strictEqual(capAFuture.layout?.position_y, 0.5);
  assert.strictEqual(capBFuture.layout?.position_y, 0.82, 'cap_b unchanged by later local edit on cap_a');

  console.log('  ✓ Layout position_x, position_y, and scale propagated strictly to spoken captions');
  console.log('  ✓ Manual text layers, overlays, and audio layers remain completely immune');
  console.log('  ✓ Word timestamps, style overrides, and animations remain 100% preserved');
  console.log('  ✓ One-time action invariant holds (future edits remain local)');
}

{
  console.log('Test 10: CapCut Style Panels Isolation (spacing, bold_italic, case)...');

  const capA = track.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_a')!;
  capA.style = {
    ...capA.style!,
    font_weight: '900',
    font_style: 'italic',
    underline: true,
    text_transform: 'title',
    letter_spacing: 3.5,
    line_height: 1.6,
    outline_opacity: 0.85,
    shadow_distance: 5.0,
    shadow_angle: 90.0,
    shadow_opacity: 0.75,
    background_mode: 'line',
    background_opacity: 0.9,
  };
  capA.layout = {
    ...capA.layout!,
    alignment: 'left',
    scale: 1.25,
  };

  // 1. Test bold_italic section
  const boldItalicResult = applySectionStylesToTrack(track, 'cap_a', 'bold_italic');
  const capBBold = boldItalicResult.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_b')!;
  assert.strictEqual(capBBold.style?.font_weight, '900', 'cap_b received font_weight 900');
  assert.strictEqual(capBBold.style?.font_style, 'italic', 'cap_b received font_style italic');
  assert.strictEqual(capBBold.style?.underline, true, 'cap_b received underline true');
  // Unrelated properties preserved
  assert.strictEqual(capBBold.style?.text_transform, 'uppercase', 'cap_b text_transform unaffected');
  assert.strictEqual(capBBold.style?.primary_color, '#FF0055', 'cap_b color unaffected');

  // 2. Test case section
  const caseResult = applySectionStylesToTrack(track, 'cap_a', 'case');
  const capBCase = caseResult.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_b')!;
  assert.strictEqual(capBCase.style?.text_transform, 'title', 'cap_b received title case');
  assert.strictEqual(capBCase.style?.font_weight, 'bold', 'cap_b font_weight untouched');

  // 3. Test spacing section
  const spacingResult = applySectionStylesToTrack(track, 'cap_a', 'spacing');
  const capBSpacing = spacingResult.nextTrack.groups.find((g) => g.id === 'cap_b')!;
  assert.strictEqual(capBSpacing.style?.letter_spacing, 3.5, 'cap_b received letter_spacing');
  assert.strictEqual(capBSpacing.style?.line_height, 1.6, 'cap_b received line_height');
  assert.strictEqual(capBSpacing.layout?.alignment, 'left', 'cap_b received alignment left');
  assert.strictEqual(capBSpacing.layout?.scale, 1.25, 'cap_b received scale 1.25');
  assert.strictEqual(capBSpacing.style?.font_size, 48, 'cap_b font_size untouched');

  console.log('  ✓ bold_italic, case, and spacing sections propagate with complete isolation');
}

============================================================
FILE: test_editor_repair_integrity.ts

import assert from 'assert';
import { CaptionTrack, CaptionGroup, Word } from './frontend/src/captions/types';
import { export_to_ass_dialogue_mock, export_to_srt_mock } from './test_mock_exporter';

console.log('🧪 Starting Dispatch Editor Repair & Integrity Test Suite...\n');

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// TEST 1: Directional Playhead Snapping & Constant Screen-Pixel Threshold
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
console.log('Test 1: Directional playhead snapping logic...');
{
  const pxPerSec = 55;
  const SNAP_PX = 12;
  const snapThreshold = SNAP_PX / pxPerSec; // ~0.218s
  const playheadTime = 10.0;
  const origDuration = 3.0;

  // Case A: Block positioned to the right of playhead (e.g. proposedStart = 10.15)
  // Directional invariant: Start edge snaps to playheadTime
  const proposedStartRight = 10.15;
  let snappedStartRight = proposedStartRight;
  if (Math.abs(proposedStartRight - playheadTime) <= snapThreshold && proposedStartRight >= playheadTime - snapThreshold) {
    snappedStartRight = playheadTime;
  }
  assert.strictEqual(snappedStartRight, 10.0, 'Block to the right of playhead must snap its start edge to playhead');

  // Case B: Block positioned to the left of playhead (e.g. proposedEnd = 9.88, so proposedStart = 6.88)
  // Directional invariant: End edge snaps to playheadTime
  const proposedEndLeft = 9.88;
  const proposedStartLeft = proposedEndLeft - origDuration;
  let snappedEndLeft = proposedEndLeft;
  let finalStartLeft = proposedStartLeft;
  if (Math.abs(proposedEndLeft - playheadTime) <= snapThreshold && proposedEndLeft <= playheadTime + snapThreshold) {
    snappedEndLeft = playheadTime;
    finalStartLeft = snappedEndLeft - origDuration;
  }
  assert.strictEqual(snappedEndLeft, 10.0, 'Block to the left of playhead must snap its end edge to playhead');
  assert.strictEqual(finalStartLeft, 7.0, 'Start edge must maintain original duration when end edge snaps');

  console.log('  ✓ Directional snapping snaps start edge when right of playhead and end edge when left of playhead');
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// TEST 2: Word Trimming Without Stretching (Speech Authenticity)
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
console.log('Test 2: Word trimming without stretching...');
{
  const origWords: Word[] = [
    { id: 'w1', text: 'Hello', start: 1.0, end: 1.5 },
    { id: 'w2', text: 'world', start: 1.6, end: 2.2 },
    { id: 'w3', text: 'this', start: 2.3, end: 2.6 },
    { id: 'w4', text: 'is', start: 2.7, end: 2.9 },
    { id: 'w5', text: 'Dispatch', start: 3.0, end: 3.8 },
  ];

  // Trim start to 2.0s:
  // Words 'Hello' (end 1.5) is excluded.
  // Word 'world' (start 1.6, end 2.2) is clamped to start at 2.0.
  // Words 'this', 'is', 'Dispatch' preserve their exact original timestamps!
  const finalTrimStart = 2.0;
  const remainingAfterStartTrim = origWords.filter((w) => w.end > finalTrimStart);
  const nextWordsStartTrim = remainingAfterStartTrim.map((w) => ({
    ...w,
    start: Math.max(finalTrimStart, w.start),
    end: Math.max(finalTrimStart, w.end),
  }));

  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsStartTrim.length, 4, 'Excluded words before trim boundary');
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsStartTrim[0].text, 'world');
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsStartTrim[0].start, 2.0, 'Boundary word clamped to trimStart');
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsStartTrim[0].end, 2.2, 'Boundary word preserves original end');
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsStartTrim[1].text, 'this');
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsStartTrim[1].start, 2.3, 'Subsequent words maintain exact authentic timestamps');
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsStartTrim[1].end, 2.6);
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsStartTrim[3].text, 'Dispatch');
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsStartTrim[3].start, 3.0);
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsStartTrim[3].end, 3.8);

  // Trim end to 2.8s:
  // Word 'Dispatch' (start 3.0) is excluded.
  // Word 'is' (start 2.7, end 2.9) is clamped to end at 2.8.
  // Word 'this' (2.3 to 2.6) preserves its original timestamps!
  const finalTrimEnd = 2.8;
  const remainingAfterEndTrim = origWords.filter((w) => w.start < finalTrimEnd);
  const nextWordsEndTrim = remainingAfterEndTrim.map((w) => ({
    ...w,
    start: Math.min(finalTrimEnd, w.start),
    end: Math.min(finalTrimEnd, w.end),
  }));

  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsEndTrim.length, 4);
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsEndTrim[3].text, 'is');
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsEndTrim[3].start, 2.7);
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsEndTrim[3].end, 2.8, 'Boundary word clamped to trimEnd');
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsEndTrim[2].text, 'this');
  assert.strictEqual(nextWordsEndTrim[2].start, 2.3, 'In-range words maintain authentic timestamps');

  console.log('  ✓ In-range speech words retain authentic timestamps without proportional stretching');
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// TEST 3: Duplicate Shifts Word Timestamps by Delta
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
console.log('Test 3: Layer duplication shifts word timestamps by delta...');
{
  const sourceGroup: CaptionGroup = {
    id: 'grp_orig',
    start: 2.0,
    end: 5.0,
    type: 'caption',
    words: [
      { id: 'w1', text: 'Step', start: 2.0, end: 2.8 },
      { id: 'w2', text: 'one', start: 3.0, end: 4.5 },
    ],
  };

  const nextStart = 2.5;
  const nextEnd = 5.5;
  const timeDelta = nextStart - sourceGroup.start; // +0.5s

  const dup: CaptionGroup = {
    ...sourceGroup,
    id: 'grp_dup',
    start: nextStart,
    end: nextEnd,
    words: (sourceGroup.words || []).map((w, idx) => ({
      ...w,
      id: `w_dup_${idx}`,
      start: Number((w.start + timeDelta).toFixed(3)),
      end: Number((w.end + timeDelta).toFixed(3)),
    })),
  };

  assert.strictEqual(dup.words![0].start, 2.5, 'Word 1 shifted by +0.5s delta');
  assert.strictEqual(dup.words![0].end, 3.3, 'Word 1 end shifted by +0.5s delta');
  assert.strictEqual(dup.words![1].start, 3.5, 'Word 2 shifted by +0.5s delta');
  assert.strictEqual(dup.words![1].end, 5.0, 'Word 2 end shifted by +0.5s delta');

  console.log('  ✓ Duplicate shifts word timestamps by exact time offset');
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// TEST 4: Literal Search & Replace in TranscriptEditor (No Regex Crashing)
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
console.log('Test 4: Literal regex search & replace...');
{
  function escapeRegex(str: string): string {
    return str.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
  }

  // Searching for literal '.' should NOT match every letter!
  const dotSearch = '.';
  const regexDot = new RegExp(escapeRegex(dotSearch), 'gi');
  const sample = 'Dispatch v1.0 is fast.';
  const replacedDot = sample.replace(regexDot, '!');
  assert.strictEqual(replacedDot, 'Dispatch v1!0 is fast!', 'Only literal periods should be replaced');

  // Searching for literal bracket '[' or '(' should not crash
  const bracketSearch = '[';
  assert.doesNotThrow(() => {
    new RegExp(escapeRegex(bracketSearch), 'gi');
  }, 'Escaping special regex character must not throw syntax error');

  console.log('  ✓ Search and Replace All handles special regex characters literally without crashing');
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// TEST 5: Exporter Protection & Non-Text Layer Subtitle Immunity
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
console.log('Test 5: Exporter skips non-text layers and hidden layers...');
{
  const mockTrack: CaptionTrack = {
    id: 'track_1',
    clip_id: 'c1',
    groups: [
      {
        id: 'g_cap',
        start: 0,
        end: 2,
        type: 'caption',
        words: [{ id: 'w1', text: 'Real subtitle', start: 0, end: 2 }],
      },
      {
        id: 'g_hidden_cap',
        start: 2,
        end: 4,
        type: 'caption',
        is_hidden: true,
        words: [{ id: 'w2', text: 'Hidden subtitle', start: 2, end: 4 }],
      },
      {
        id: 'g_audio',
        start: 0,
        end: 5,
        type: 'audio',
        audio_url: '/clips/audio.mp3',
        words: [],
      },
      {
        id: 'g_overlay',
        start: 1,
        end: 3,
        type: 'overlay',
        overlay_url: '/clips/overlay.png',
        words: [],
      },
    ],
    default_style: { font_family: 'Montserrat' },
    default_layout: { position_x: 0.5, position_y: 0.78 },
    default_animation: { type: 'pop' },
  };

  const assDialogue = export_to_ass_dialogue_mock(mockTrack);
  const srtSubtitles = export_to_srt_mock(mockTrack);

  assert.strictEqual(assDialogue.length, 1, 'Only the active, non-hidden caption should be in ASS dialogue');
  assert.strictEqual(assDialogue[0].text, 'Real subtitle');

  assert.strictEqual(srtSubtitles.length, 1, 'Only the active, non-hidden caption should be in SRT output');
  assert.strictEqual(srtSubtitles[0].text, 'Real subtitle');

  console.log('  ✓ Non-text layers and hidden layers are 100% excluded from exported subtitles');
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// TEST 6: Dynamic Multi-track Non-Overlapping Allocation
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
console.log('Test 6: Multi-track allocation with unpinned layers...');
{
  const groups: CaptionGroup[] = [
    { id: 'g1', start: 0, end: 5, type: 'caption' },
    { id: 'g2', start: 2, end: 7, type: 'text' }, // Overlaps g1 -> should be allocated to Track L2
    { id: 'g3', start: 6, end: 10, type: 'caption' }, // Does not overlap g1 -> can pack into Track L1
  ];

  const sorted = [...groups].sort((a, b) => a.start - b.start);
  const buckets: CaptionGroup[][] = [];

  sorted.forEach((grp) => {
    let placed = false;
    for (let i = 0; i < buckets.length; i++) {
      const bucket = buckets[i];
      const hasOverlap = bucket.some(
        (existing) => Math.max(grp.start, existing.start) < Math.min(grp.end, existing.end)
      );
      if (!hasOverlap) {
        bucket.push(grp);
        placed = true;
        break;
      }
    }
    if (!placed) {
      buckets.push([grp]);
    }
  });

  assert.strictEqual(buckets.length, 2, 'Should create exactly 2 tracks for 2 overlapping spans');
  assert.deepStrictEqual(buckets[0].map((g) => g.id), ['g1', 'g3'], 'Non-overlapping g1 and g3 pack into track 0');
  assert.deepStrictEqual(buckets[1].map((g) => g.id), ['g2'], 'Overlapping g2 packs into track 1');

  console.log('  ✓ Dynamic track allocator packs non-overlapping elements cleanly without forced collisions');
}

console.log('\n🎉 ALL 6 EDITOR INTEGRITY & REPAIR TESTS PASSED 100%!');

FILE: test_mock_exporter.ts

import { CaptionTrack } from './frontend/src/captions/types';

export function export_to_ass_dialogue_mock(track: CaptionTrack): { text: string; start: number; end: number }[] {
  const events: { text: string; start: number; end: number }[] = [];
  for (const group of track.groups || []) {
    // Non-text layers and hidden layers are never rendered or exported as subtitles
    if (group.type === 'audio' || group.type === 'voiceover' || group.type === 'overlay' || group.is_hidden) {
      continue;
    }
    const text = (group.words || []).map((w) => w.text).join(' ');
    if (text) {
      events.push({ text, start: group.start, end: group.end });
    }
  }
  return events;
}

export function export_to_srt_mock(track: CaptionTrack): { text: string; start: number; end: number }[] {
  const subtitles: { text: string; start: number; end: number }[] = [];
  for (const group of track.groups || []) {
    if (group.type === 'audio' || group.type === 'voiceover' || group.type === 'overlay' || group.is_hidden) {
      continue;
    }
    const text = (group.words || []).map((w) => w.text).join(' ');
    if (text) {
      subtitles.push({ text, start: group.start, end: group.end });
    }
  }
  return subtitles;
}

import assert from 'node:assert';
import { CaptionTrack, CaptionGroup } from './frontend/src/captions/types';
import { applyColorOpacity, applyCaseTransform } from './frontend/src/captions/DispatchCaptionRenderer';

console.log('🧪 Starting Dispatch "Text vs Caption Separation & Performance" Test Suite...\n');

// 1. Data Model & Highlight Immunity Test
console.log('Test 1: Normal text fill & spoken-word highlight immunity...');
{
  const textGroup: CaptionGroup = {
    id: 'txt_1',
    type: 'text',
    start: 1.0,
    end: 4.0,
    style: {
      primary_color: '#38BDF8',
      highlight_color: '#FFE600',
      highlight_enabled: false,
      font_family: 'Montserrat',
      font_size: 40,
    },
    words: [
      { id: 'w1', text: 'Brand', start: 1.0, end: 2.0 },
      { id: 'w2', text: 'Watermark', start: 2.0, end: 4.0 },
    ],
  };

  const captionGroup: CaptionGroup = {
    id: 'cap_1',
    type: 'caption',
    start: 1.0,
    end: 4.0,
    style: {
      primary_color: '#FFFFFF',
      highlight_color: '#FFE600',
      highlight_enabled: true,
      font_family: 'Montserrat',
      font_size: 44,
    },
    words: [
      { id: 'w3', text: 'Hello', start: 1.0, end: 2.0 },
      { id: 'w4', text: 'World', start: 2.0, end: 4.0 },
    ],
  };

  // Helper matching the renderer resolution logic
  function resolveWordVisual(grp: CaptionGroup, currentTime: number, wordIdx: number) {
    const isCaption = (grp.type || 'caption') === 'caption';
    const highlightEnabled = isCaption && (grp.style?.highlight_enabled !== false);
    const word = grp.words![wordIdx];
    const isSpoken = currentTime >= word.start && currentTime <= word.end;

    let textColor = grp.style?.primary_color || '#FFFFFF';
    if (isCaption && isSpoken && highlightEnabled) {
      textColor = word.style_override?.highlight_color || grp.style?.highlight_color || '#FFE600';
    } else if (word.style_override?.color) {
      textColor = word.style_override.color;
    }

    const animType = grp.animation?.active_word?.type || grp.animation?.type;
    const isPunched = isCaption && isSpoken && animType && (animType === 'pop' || animType === 'bounce');
    const activeScale = isPunched ? (grp.animation?.active_scale || 1.15) : 1.0;

    return { textColor, activeScale, isCaption, highlightEnabled };
  }

  // At time 1.5s, word 0 is active for both
  const textAt15 = resolveWordVisual(textGroup, 1.5, 0);
  assert.strictEqual(textAt15.isCaption, false, 'textGroup must not be classified as caption');
  assert.strictEqual(textAt15.highlightEnabled, false, 'textGroup must never have highlightEnabled');
  assert.strictEqual(textAt15.textColor, '#38BDF8', 'Normal text must preserve primary fill color even during spoken timestamp');
  assert.strictEqual(textAt15.activeScale, 1.0, 'Normal text must never punch or scale pop');

  const captionAt15 = resolveWordVisual(captionGroup, 1.5, 0);
  assert.strictEqual(captionAt15.isCaption, true, 'captionGroup must be classified as caption');
  assert.strictEqual(captionAt15.highlightEnabled, true, 'captionGroup must have highlightEnabled');
  assert.strictEqual(captionAt15.textColor, '#FFE600', 'Caption must illuminate with highlight_color when active');

  console.log('  ✓ Normal text preserves primary_color and remains immune to spoken word highlight and pop punch');
  console.log('  ✓ Caption correctly highlights with highlight_color on active words');
}

// 2. Caption Highlight Toggle Test
console.log('Test 2: Caption highlight toggle (uniform phrase styling)...');
{
  const captionWithToggleOff: CaptionGroup = {
    id: 'cap_off',
    type: 'caption',
    start: 0,
    end: 3.0,
    style: {
      primary_color: '#FFFFFF',

    },
    words: [
      { id: 'w1', text: 'Uniform', start: 0, end: 1.5 },
      { id: 'w2', text: 'Text', start: 1.5, end: 3.0 },
    ],

  const isCaption = (captionWithToggleOff.type || 'caption') === 'caption';
  const highlightEnabled = isCaption && (captionWithToggleOff.style?.highlight_enabled !== false);
  assert.strictEqual(highlightEnabled, false, 'Caption with highlight_enabled=false must not highlight');

  const word = captionWithToggleOff.words![0];
  const isSpoken = 1.0 >= word.start && 1.0 <= word.end;
  let textColor = captionWithToggleOff.style?.primary_color || '#FFFFFF';
  if (isCaption && isSpoken && highlightEnabled) {
    textColor = captionWithToggleOff.style?.highlight_color || '#FFE600';
  }

  assert.strictEqual(textColor, '#FFFFFF', 'Caption with highlight disabled must render primary_color for spoken word');
  console.log('  ✓ Caption with highlight_enabled: false maintains uniform primary fill color without highlight');
}

// 3. Multi-layer Concurrent Rendering Test (No Single-Group Swallow Bug)
console.log('Test 3: Multi-layer concurrent rendering (caption + watermark simultaneously visible)...');
{
  const multiTrack: CaptionTrack = {
    version: 2,
    language: 'en',
    groups: [
      {
        id: 'cap_subtitle',
        type: 'caption',
        start: 0.0,
        end: 5.0,
        words: [{ id: 'w1', text: 'Subtitle', start: 0, end: 5.0 }],
        layout: { position_y: 0.8 },
      },
      {
        id: 'txt_watermark',
        type: 'text',
        start: 0.0,
        end: 10.0,
        words: [{ id: 'w2', text: 'Watermark', start: 0, end: 10.0 }],
        layout: { position_y: 0.1 },
      },
      {
        id: 'aud_bgm',
        type: 'audio',
        start: 0.0,
        end: 10.0,
        words: [],
      },
    ],
  };

  const currentTime = 2.5;

  // Multi-group filter logic from updated DispatchCaptionRenderer and InteractiveTextCanvas
  const activeRenderGroups = multiTrack.groups.filter(
    (grp) => grp.type !== 'audio' && grp.type !== 'voiceover' && currentTime >= grp.start && currentTime <= grp.end
  );

  assert.strictEqual(activeRenderGroups.length, 2, 'Both caption and text layers must be active simultaneously');
  const activeIds = activeRenderGroups.map((g) => g.id);
  assert.ok(activeIds.includes('cap_subtitle'), 'Subtitle caption must be rendered');
  assert.ok(activeIds.includes('txt_watermark'), 'Watermark text must be rendered alongside subtitle');
  assert.ok(!activeIds.includes('aud_bgm'), 'Audio track must never be rendered on canvas');

  console.log('  ✓ Multi-layer composition renders both caption and text layers concurrently without swallowing');
}

// 4. Performance Comparator Immunity Test (Suppresses 30fps Re-rendering)
console.log('Test 4: CaptionStudio React.memo comparator performance verification...');
{
  function areCaptionStudioPropsEqual(prev: any, next: any): boolean {
    if (next.activePanel === 'transcript') {
      if (prev.currentTime !== next.currentTime) return false;
    }

    if (prev.track !== next.track) return false;
    if (prev.selectedGroupId !== next.selectedGroupId) return false;
    if (prev.isVideoSelected !== next.isVideoSelected) return false;
    if (prev.layoutMode !== next.layoutMode) return false;
    if (prev.mainVideoVolume !== next.mainVideoVolume) return false;
    if (prev.isMainVideoMuted !== next.isMainVideoMuted) return false;
    if (prev.activePanel !== next.activePanel) return false;
    if (prev.duration !== next.duration) return false;
    if (prev.canUndo !== next.canUndo) return false;
    if (prev.canRedo !== next.canRedo) return false;

    return true;
  }

  const baseProps = {
    track: { version: 2, groups: [] },
    currentTime: 1.0,
    selectedGroupId: 'txt_1',
    isVideoSelected: false,
    layoutMode: 'crop_916',
    mainVideoVolume: 100,
    isMainVideoMuted: false,
    activePanel: 'style',
    duration: 30,
    canUndo: false,
    canRedo: false,
  };

  // 1. Playhead advances from 1.0 to 1.033 (30fps playback tick) while editing Style panel
  const playheadNext = { ...baseProps, currentTime: 1.033 };
  const shouldSkipReRender = areCaptionStudioPropsEqual(baseProps, playheadNext);
  assert.strictEqual(shouldSkipReRender, true, 'CaptionStudio must suppress re-rendering on frame ticks when transcript is closed');

  // 2. User selects a different layer
  const selectNext = { ...baseProps, selectedGroupId: 'cap_2' };
  assert.strictEqual(areCaptionStudioPropsEqual(baseProps, selectNext), false, 'Must re-render when selected layer changes');

  // 3. User switches inspector tabs
  const tabNext = { ...baseProps, activePanel: 'fonts' };
  assert.strictEqual(areCaptionStudioPropsEqual(baseProps, tabNext), false, 'Must re-render when active panel changes');

  // 4. In Transcript editor mode, playhead ticks MUST re-render for live karaoke scrolling
  const transcriptPrev = { ...baseProps, activePanel: 'transcript', currentTime: 1.0 };
  const transcriptNext = { ...baseProps, activePanel: 'transcript', currentTime: 1.033 };
  assert.strictEqual(areCaptionStudioPropsEqual(transcriptPrev, transcriptNext), false, 'Transcript mode must re-render on time ticks for live karaoke');

  console.log('  ✓ 30fps currentTime ticks are cleanly suppressed during normal playback & editing');
  console.log('  ✓ Layer selection & inspector tab switching immediately trigger re-render');
  console.log('  ✓ Transcript editor mode selectively receives time ticks for fluid karaoke tracking');
}

console.log('\n🎉 ALL TEXT/CAPTION SEPARATION & PERFORMANCE TESTS PASSED 100%!');
